        Idea:
# Lift Standard Review Quality via Mechanical Pre-checks, Flag Re-verification, and Critique Re-tiering

## Context

Megaplan has three robustness modes: `light`, `standard`, `heavy`. Heavy mode just shipped a new parallel `run_parallel_review` with 4 focused LLM checks + mechanical pre-checks. Standard mode still runs a single `_review_prompt()` call that anchors to `finalize.json` (what the plan said to do), not the original issue. We've validated that the questions standard mode SHOULD be asking are already present in the critique phase (`megaplan/checks.py`) but are gated to extended tier or run only against the plan, not the post-execute diff.

This plan is a targeted, **cheap** lift of standard mode quality. No new LLM calls in standard mode. Light mode stays exactly as today. Heavy mode mechanical pre-checks are preserved.

## Goal

Three changes that make standard-mode critique and review materially more thorough without adding API cost:

1. **Run mechanical pre-checks in both standard and heavy review**, not only heavy. Attach as context in standard; auto-block clear failures.
2. **Add a "re-verify critique flags against the final diff" section** to the standard `_review_prompt()` so the single review call re-checks whether pre-execute concerns were actually resolved by the post-execute code.
3. **Re-tier critique checks**: promote `all_locations` and `callers` from extended to core, demote `criteria_quality` from extended-core ordering (or remove from core if it's there). Standard-mode critique becomes substantially more thorough at zero extra cost per check since it's a swap, not an addition.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review` — branches on robustness. Standard path runs the legacy single-call worker. Heavy path calls `run_pre_checks` then `run_parallel_review`. The heavy branch at ~line 1251 calls `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))`.
- `megaplan/review_mechanical.py::run_pre_checks` — returns a list of advisory flag dicts. Runs instantly (no LLM). Safe to call multiple times.
- `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt` — the single-call standard review prompt. Produces criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts/review_verdict/rework_items. Must stay byte-identical in light mode but can gain new sections in standard mode.
- `megaplan/checks.py::CRITIQUE_CHECKS` — 8 critique checks with `tier` in {"core", "extended"}. Standard robustness runs core only via `checks_for_robustness("standard")`. Heavy runs all 8.
- `megaplan/_core/flags.py` (or wherever flag registry lives) — the flag registry with open/verified/disputed status per flag. Already accessible from review.
- `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py` — review schema. Already widened in the previous robust-review megaplan to accept `pre_check_flags`. The standard review payload must remain backward compatible.

## What needs to exist

### 1. Run mechanical pre-checks in standard mode too

In `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review`, move the `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))` call out of the heavy branch and run it **unconditionally** before both the standard and heavy review paths.

- For heavy: keep the existing flow — `pre_check_flags` becomes the input to `run_parallel_review`, gets merged into `merged_payload["pre_check_flags"]`.
- For standard: after `run_pre_checks` returns, filter for **blocking** flags (clear failures like `source_touch` reporting a test-only diff, or `dead_guard_static` reporting an unreachable guard in a new source branch). If any blocking flag exists, short-circuit: write a minimal `review.json` with `review_verdict="needs_rework"`, populate `rework_items` with one entry per blocking mechanical flag, set `pre_check_flags` to the full list, and skip the LLM review call entirely. This is a free auto-block.
- For standard with no blocking mechanical flags: attach `pre_check_flags` to the existing review payload as context for the single review call. Pass them into `_review_prompt` as an extra block so the LLM can see them when scoring criteria. Populate `payload["pre_check_flags"]` from the returned worker result.
- Light mode: do NOT run mechanical pre-checks. Light mode stays exactly as today.

Decide which pre-check findings are "blocking" via a helper:
- `source_touch` with severity `significant` and "only touches test-like files" detail → blocking
- `dead_guard_static` with `UNREACHABLE` in the id → blocking (requires a new severity tier or id marker since current implementation only reports parse failures)
- `diff_size_sanity` → never blocking (advisory only)

If the distinction is hard to make cleanly, the simpler alternative is: in standard mode, all mechanical flags are advisory only and attached as prompt context; no auto-blocking. Auto-blocking can come in a later PR. Implementer's call — pick the simpler option if the blocking logic requires more than a single-function helper.

### 2. Re-verify critique flags against final diff in standard review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt`, add a new section between the existing requirements block and the JSON shape spec. The new section should:

- Read the flag registry via `load_flag_registry(plan_dir)`.
- Filter for flags with status `open`, `verified`, or `disputed` (not `resolved`).
- For each such flag, include its id, concern text, severity, and current status in the prompt.
- Instruct the model: "For each flag below that was raised during critique, verify whether the final diff actually addresses the concern. A flag is resolved if the final diff contains code that directly addresses the concern. If a flag is not resolved in the final diff, add a `rework_item` referencing the flag id and explaining what's still missing. Do not trust pre-execute promises — check the diff itself."
- The output `rework_items` gain a new optional field `flag_id` pointing to the critique flag id. Existing `source` field should be set to `"review_flag_reverify"` for any rework item synthesized this way.

Schema impact: add optional `flag_id` field to rework_items shape in both `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py`. Keep it optional so existing payloads still validate.

The new prompt section only runs if there are open/verified/disputed flags in the registry; if the registry is empty or all resolved, the section is omitted entirely (keeps the standard prompt byte-identical for plans without critique flags, which is important for the existing byte-identical regression test in `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`).

This must not change light mode. Light mode doesn't run critique at all and has no flag registry, so a "no flags → no section" gate handles this automatically.

### 3. Re-tier critique checks

In `megaplan/checks.py`, in `CRITIQUE_CHECKS`:

- Promote `all_locations` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Promote `callers` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Demote `criteria_quality` from `tier="core"` to `tier="extended"` (if it's core — verify first). The reasoning: `criteria_quality` is about whether success criteria are well-formed, which is plan hygiene. `all_locations` and `callers` are about whether the plan actually solves the bug, which is more important for standard mode.

This is a **net-zero change** in number of checks per mode:
- Standard robustness was running 4 core checks, will still run 4 (swap `criteria_quality` out, swap `all_locations` in). Net neutral, and if `callers` is also promoted, standard gains one check — verify against the brief's "no new cost per check" claim and either: (a) accept a 1-check increase in standard (minor cost bump), or (b) also demote one more current core check to keep count at 4. The simpler choice is (a) — 5 core checks in standard is fine.
- Heavy robustness is unchanged in total count (still 8 checks, same set, just different tier labels).

Update `megaplan/checks.py:140-144` (or wherever the `checks_for_robustness()` function lives) — no code change should be needed since it filters by tier; only the tier labels on the check entries change.

Update any tests that assert the specific set of core checks to match the new tiering.

### 4. Tests

- **`tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`**:
  - Add a test that standard mode calls `run_pre_checks` before the single review worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with a blocking mechanical flag (if auto-blocking is implemented) short-circuits and writes a `needs_rework` review.json without calling the worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with no blocking mechanical flags attaches `pre_check_flags` to the payload.
  - The existing byte-identical standard-mode fixture test should either continue passing (if no flag registry present) or be updated to expect the new `pre_check_flags` key in the payload. Pick whichever is less churn; document the choice in the test docstring.

- **`tests/test_prompts.py`**:
  - Add a grep-assertion test that `_review_prompt` output includes the flag-reverify section when a fixture flag registry has open flags.
  - Add a symmetric test that the section is absent when the registry is empty (byte-identical backward compat).

- **`tests/test_checks.py`**:
  - Update the expected core/extended tier sets to match the new tiering.
  - Assert `all_locations` and `callers` are in core.
  - Assert `criteria_quality` is in extended.

- **`tests/test_schemas.py`**:
  - Add a test case for `rework_items[].flag_id` (optional string).

- **Regression**: full suite should still pass. Critically: `tests/test_parallel_critique.py`, `tests/test_parallel_review.py`, `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`, `tests/test_review_checks.py`, `tests/test_review_mechanical.py`.

## Out of scope

- Any changes to `run_parallel_review`, `review_checks.py`, or `review_mechanical.py` internals.
- Any changes to heavy mode behavior beyond preserving the existing pre-check plumbing.
- Any changes to light mode.
- New LLM-based checks.
- Changes to critique check guidance text.
- Auto-blocking in standard mode based on mechanical flags — implement if easy, skip if not.

## Success criteria

- Standard mode runs `run_pre_checks` and attaches `pre_check_flags` to its review payload.
- Standard mode's review prompt includes a flag-reverify section when open critique flags exist.
- `all_locations` and `callers` are now core-tier critique checks.
- Light mode review is byte-identical to today.
- Heavy mode review is unchanged in structure.
- All existing tests pass; new tests for the three changes pass.
- No new LLM calls are added to standard mode — the changes are: (a) mechanical pre-checks (free), (b) a prompt section (no new call), (c) check re-tiering (swap, not add).

## Files likely to change

- `megaplan/handlers.py` — `handle_review` changes
- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — `_review_prompt` changes
- `megaplan/checks.py` — tier re-labeling
- `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` — optional `flag_id` in rework_items
- `megaplan/schemas.py` — mirror the schema change
- `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py` — new tests
- `tests/test_prompts.py` — new grep-assertions
- `tests/test_checks.py` — updated tier assertions
- `tests/test_schemas.py` — flag_id schema test

  "meta_commentary": "Three coordinated changes to the standard-mode review path. Order matters: do T1 (label-only tier swap, instant feedback via T5) and T2 (schema) first because they're free; then T3 (`_review_prompt` extension) which T4 depends on; then T4 (handler wiring + persistence). Land tests alongside their corresponding code change, not at the end \u2014 the per-step regression catches issues early.\n\nCritical correctness pitfalls: (1) The re-verify filter MUST include `addressed` status (FLAG-001) \u2014 without it the feature is silently broken because `update_flags_after_revise` writes that exact status. (2) Standard branch MUST call `update_flags_after_review` (FLAG-002), light mode must NOT \u2014 this asymmetry preserves byte-identity. (3) Re-tiering grows standard critique from 4\u21925 LLM calls (not 6) because `verification` is demoted as compensation; `criteria_quality` is already extended (D3). (4) `tests/test_parallel_critique.py:118-120` will break on the count change (FLAG-004) \u2014 fix proactively in T6, don't wait for it to fail.\n\nByte-identity contract for light mode is load-bearing and tested two ways: T8(c) snapshots `_review_prompt` output, T9(b) snapshots `review.json` shape. If either fails, the fix is in the gating logic (T3's `extra_sections` empty-string path or T4's `if robustness == 'standard'` guards), NOT in the test.\n\nWorker plumbing in T4 step 5 is the riskiest unknown. Investigate `megaplan/workers.py` first to confirm whether `_review_prompt` is called with kwargs or via a fixed signature. Sidecar fallback (`pre_check_flags.json` next to the registry) is acceptable per the plan and only adds one read; don't waste cycles on a refactor.\n\n`update_flags_after_review` may expect `payload['checks']` \u2014 standard payloads don't have it. Verify this is a soft-fail/no-op before assuming it works. If it raises, the minimal fix is a `payload.setdefault('checks', [])` or equivalent guard at the call site.\n\nAuto-blocking on mechanical flags in standard mode is explicitly deferred (D1) \u2014 leave a one-line comment at the attachment site noting this. Don't be tempted to add it.\n\nFinal validation in T10 must run the focused tests first (fast feedback), then brief-mandated regressions, then full suite. The throwaway sanity script is a belt-and-braces check on the kwarg signature and tier counts \u2014 delete it after running.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] criteria-prompt: criteria prompt: v3 replaces `_review_prompt()` in heavy mode with a new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, so the plan no longer follows the brief's explicit requirement that `_review_prompt()` remain the heavy-mode `criteria_verdict` check. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname="hidden md:block"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still contradicts itself on mobile behavior. assumption #3 says the browse-mode mobile score toggle will be preserved, but step 4.9 removes the entire mobile sticky nav that contains that toggle in `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:314-330`. the implementer cannot satisfy both instructions as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic. - [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.         Idea:
# Lift Standard Review Quality via Mechanical Pre-checks, Flag Re-verification, and Critique Re-tiering

## Context

Megaplan has three robustness modes: `light`, `standard`, `heavy`. Heavy mode just shipped a new parallel `run_parallel_review` with 4 focused LLM checks + mechanical pre-checks. Standard mode still runs a single `_review_prompt()` call that anchors to `finalize.json` (what the plan said to do), not the original issue. We've validated that the questions standard mode SHOULD be asking are already present in the critique phase (`megaplan/checks.py`) but are gated to extended tier or run only against the plan, not the post-execute diff.

This plan is a targeted, **cheap** lift of standard mode quality. No new LLM calls in standard mode. Light mode stays exactly as today. Heavy mode mechanical pre-checks are preserved.

## Goal

Three changes that make standard-mode critique and review materially more thorough without adding API cost:

1. **Run mechanical pre-checks in both standard and heavy review**, not only heavy. Attach as context in standard; auto-block clear failures.
2. **Add a "re-verify critique flags against the final diff" section** to the standard `_review_prompt()` so the single review call re-checks whether pre-execute concerns were actually resolved by the post-execute code.
3. **Re-tier critique checks**: promote `all_locations` and `callers` from extended to core, demote `criteria_quality` from extended-core ordering (or remove from core if it's there). Standard-mode critique becomes substantially more thorough at zero extra cost per check since it's a swap, not an addition.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review` — branches on robustness. Standard path runs the legacy single-call worker. Heavy path calls `run_pre_checks` then `run_parallel_review`. The heavy branch at ~line 1251 calls `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))`.
- `megaplan/review_mechanical.py::run_pre_checks` — returns a list of advisory flag dicts. Runs instantly (no LLM). Safe to call multiple times.
- `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt` — the single-call standard review prompt. Produces criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts/review_verdict/rework_items. Must stay byte-identical in light mode but can gain new sections in standard mode.
- `megaplan/checks.py::CRITIQUE_CHECKS` — 8 critique checks with `tier` in {"core", "extended"}. Standard robustness runs core only via `checks_for_robustness("standard")`. Heavy runs all 8.
- `megaplan/_core/flags.py` (or wherever flag registry lives) — the flag registry with open/verified/disputed status per flag. Already accessible from review.
- `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py` — review schema. Already widened in the previous robust-review megaplan to accept `pre_check_flags`. The standard review payload must remain backward compatible.

## What needs to exist

### 1. Run mechanical pre-checks in standard mode too

In `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review`, move the `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))` call out of the heavy branch and run it **unconditionally** before both the standard and heavy review paths.

- For heavy: keep the existing flow — `pre_check_flags` becomes the input to `run_parallel_review`, gets merged into `merged_payload["pre_check_flags"]`.
- For standard: after `run_pre_checks` returns, filter for **blocking** flags (clear failures like `source_touch` reporting a test-only diff, or `dead_guard_static` reporting an unreachable guard in a new source branch). If any blocking flag exists, short-circuit: write a minimal `review.json` with `review_verdict="needs_rework"`, populate `rework_items` with one entry per blocking mechanical flag, set `pre_check_flags` to the full list, and skip the LLM review call entirely. This is a free auto-block.
- For standard with no blocking mechanical flags: attach `pre_check_flags` to the existing review payload as context for the single review call. Pass them into `_review_prompt` as an extra block so the LLM can see them when scoring criteria. Populate `payload["pre_check_flags"]` from the returned worker result.
- Light mode: do NOT run mechanical pre-checks. Light mode stays exactly as today.

Decide which pre-check findings are "blocking" via a helper:
- `source_touch` with severity `significant` and "only touches test-like files" detail → blocking
- `dead_guard_static` with `UNREACHABLE` in the id → blocking (requires a new severity tier or id marker since current implementation only reports parse failures)
- `diff_size_sanity` → never blocking (advisory only)

If the distinction is hard to make cleanly, the simpler alternative is: in standard mode, all mechanical flags are advisory only and attached as prompt context; no auto-blocking. Auto-blocking can come in a later PR. Implementer's call — pick the simpler option if the blocking logic requires more than a single-function helper.

### 2. Re-verify critique flags against final diff in standard review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt`, add a new section between the existing requirements block and the JSON shape spec. The new section should:

- Read the flag registry via `load_flag_registry(plan_dir)`.
- Filter for flags with status `open`, `verified`, or `disputed` (not `resolved`).
- For each such flag, include its id, concern text, severity, and current status in the prompt.
- Instruct the model: "For each flag below that was raised during critique, verify whether the final diff actually addresses the concern. A flag is resolved if the final diff contains code that directly addresses the concern. If a flag is not resolved in the final diff, add a `rework_item` referencing the flag id and explaining what's still missing. Do not trust pre-execute promises — check the diff itself."
- The output `rework_items` gain a new optional field `flag_id` pointing to the critique flag id. Existing `source` field should be set to `"review_flag_reverify"` for any rework item synthesized this way.

Schema impact: add optional `flag_id` field to rework_items shape in both `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py`. Keep it optional so existing payloads still validate.

The new prompt section only runs if there are open/verified/disputed flags in the registry; if the registry is empty or all resolved, the section is omitted entirely (keeps the standard prompt byte-identical for plans without critique flags, which is important for the existing byte-identical regression test in `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`).

This must not change light mode. Light mode doesn't run critique at all and has no flag registry, so a "no flags → no section" gate handles this automatically.

### 3. Re-tier critique checks

In `megaplan/checks.py`, in `CRITIQUE_CHECKS`:

- Promote `all_locations` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Promote `callers` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Demote `criteria_quality` from `tier="core"` to `tier="extended"` (if it's core — verify first). The reasoning: `criteria_quality` is about whether success criteria are well-formed, which is plan hygiene. `all_locations` and `callers` are about whether the plan actually solves the bug, which is more important for standard mode.

This is a **net-zero change** in number of checks per mode:
- Standard robustness was running 4 core checks, will still run 4 (swap `criteria_quality` out, swap `all_locations` in). Net neutral, and if `callers` is also promoted, standard gains one check — verify against the brief's "no new cost per check" claim and either: (a) accept a 1-check increase in standard (minor cost bump), or (b) also demote one more current core check to keep count at 4. The simpler choice is (a) — 5 core checks in standard is fine.
- Heavy robustness is unchanged in total count (still 8 checks, same set, just different tier labels).

Update `megaplan/checks.py:140-144` (or wherever the `checks_for_robustness()` function lives) — no code change should be needed since it filters by tier; only the tier labels on the check entries change.

Update any tests that assert the specific set of core checks to match the new tiering.

### 4. Tests

- **`tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`**:
  - Add a test that standard mode calls `run_pre_checks` before the single review worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with a blocking mechanical flag (if auto-blocking is implemented) short-circuits and writes a `needs_rework` review.json without calling the worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with no blocking mechanical flags attaches `pre_check_flags` to the payload.
  - The existing byte-identical standard-mode fixture test should either continue passing (if no flag registry present) or be updated to expect the new `pre_check_flags` key in the payload. Pick whichever is less churn; document the choice in the test docstring.

- **`tests/test_prompts.py`**:
  - Add a grep-assertion test that `_review_prompt` output includes the flag-reverify section when a fixture flag registry has open flags.
  - Add a symmetric test that the section is absent when the registry is empty (byte-identical backward compat).

- **`tests/test_checks.py`**:
  - Update the expected core/extended tier sets to match the new tiering.
  - Assert `all_locations` and `callers` are in core.
  - Assert `criteria_quality` is in extended.

- **`tests/test_schemas.py`**:
  - Add a test case for `rework_items[].flag_id` (optional string).

- **Regression**: full suite should still pass. Critically: `tests/test_parallel_critique.py`, `tests/test_parallel_review.py`, `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`, `tests/test_review_checks.py`, `tests/test_review_mechanical.py`.

## Out of scope

- Any changes to `run_parallel_review`, `review_checks.py`, or `review_mechanical.py` internals.
- Any changes to heavy mode behavior beyond preserving the existing pre-check plumbing.
- Any changes to light mode.
- New LLM-based checks.
- Changes to critique check guidance text.
- Auto-blocking in standard mode based on mechanical flags — implement if easy, skip if not.

## Success criteria

- Standard mode runs `run_pre_checks` and attaches `pre_check_flags` to its review payload.
- Standard mode's review prompt includes a flag-reverify section when open critique flags exist.
- `all_locations` and `callers` are now core-tier critique checks.
- Light mode review is byte-identical to today.
- Heavy mode review is unchanged in structure.
- All existing tests pass; new tests for the three changes pass.
- No new LLM calls are added to standard mode — the changes are: (a) mechanical pre-checks (free), (b) a prompt section (no new call), (c) check re-tiering (swap, not add).

## Files likely to change

- `megaplan/handlers.py` — `handle_review` changes
- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — `_review_prompt` changes
- `megaplan/checks.py` — tier re-labeling
- `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` — optional `flag_id` in rework_items
- `megaplan/schemas.py` — mirror the schema change
- `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py` — new tests
- `tests/test_prompts.py` — new grep-assertions
- `tests/test_checks.py` — updated tier assertions
- `tests/test_schemas.py` — flag_id schema test

  "meta_commentary": "Three coordinated changes to the standard-mode review path. Order matters: do T1 (label-only tier swap, instant feedback via T5) and T2 (schema) first because they're free; then T3 (`_review_prompt` extension) which T4 depends on; then T4 (handler wiring + persistence). Land tests alongside their corresponding code change, not at the end \u2014 the per-step regression catches issues early.\n\nCritical correctness pitfalls: (1) The re-verify filter MUST include `addressed` status (FLAG-001) \u2014 without it the feature is silently broken because `update_flags_after_revise` writes that exact status. (2) Standard branch MUST call `update_flags_after_review` (FLAG-002), light mode must NOT \u2014 this asymmetry preserves byte-identity. (3) Re-tiering grows standard critique from 4\u21925 LLM calls (not 6) because `verification` is demoted as compensation; `criteria_quality` is already extended (D3). (4) `tests/test_parallel_critique.py:118-120` will break on the count change (FLAG-004) \u2014 fix proactively in T6, don't wait for it to fail.\n\nByte-identity contract for light mode is load-bearing and tested two ways: T8(c) snapshots `_review_prompt` output, T9(b) snapshots `review.json` shape. If either fails, the fix is in the gating logic (T3's `extra_sections` empty-string path or T4's `if robustness == 'standard'` guards), NOT in the test.\n\nWorker plumbing in T4 step 5 is the riskiest unknown. Investigate `megaplan/workers.py` first to confirm whether `_review_prompt` is called with kwargs or via a fixed signature. Sidecar fallback (`pre_check_flags.json` next to the registry) is acceptable per the plan and only adds one read; don't waste cycles on a refactor.\n\n`update_flags_after_review` may expect `payload['checks']` \u2014 standard payloads don't have it. Verify this is a soft-fail/no-op before assuming it works. If it raises, the minimal fix is a `payload.setdefault('checks', [])` or equivalent guard at the call site.\n\nAuto-blocking on mechanical flags in standard mode is explicitly deferred (D1) \u2014 leave a one-line comment at the attachment site noting this. Don't be tempted to add it.\n\nFinal validation in T10 must run the focused tests first (fast feedback), then brief-mandated regressions, then full suite. The throwaway sanity script is a belt-and-braces check on the kwarg signature and tier counts \u2014 delete it after running.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] criteria-prompt: criteria prompt: v3 replaces `_review_prompt()` in heavy mode with a new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, so the plan no longer follows the brief's explicit requirement that `_review_prompt()` remain the heavy-mode `criteria_verdict` check. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname="hidden md:block"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still contradicts itself on mobile behavior. assumption #3 says the browse-mode mobile score toggle will be preserved, but step 4.9 removes the entire mobile sticky nav that contains that toggle in `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:314-330`. the implementer cannot satisfy both instructions as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked step 7 against the brief's explicit prompt-builder requirement. the revision fixes the old context leak by introducing a brand-new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, but the brief said `_review_prompt()` should stay as-is and still run as the `criteria_verdict` check in heavy mode; v3 solves the problem by replacing that heavy-mode criteria path instead of reusing the baseline review prompt, which is still a material divergence from the requested design. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] diff-capture: diff capture: the proposed `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` helper uses `git diff head`, which will miss untracked files even though the existing execution-evidence path already treats untracked files as real changed work. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-phase-contract: early-killed baselines pass truncated output to the execute worker, which may produce lower-quality diagnosis than full output. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:\n           - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to\n           - `issue`: what is wrong\n           - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like\n           - `actual`: what was observed\n           - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding\n+          - Include optional `flag_id` and `source` fields when the issue comes from critique-flag re-verification (use `source: \"review_flag_reverify\"`).\n         - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility).         Idea:
# Lift Standard Review Quality via Mechanical Pre-checks, Flag Re-verification, and Critique Re-tiering

## Context

Megaplan has three robustness modes: `light`, `standard`, `heavy`. Heavy mode just shipped a new parallel `run_parallel_review` with 4 focused LLM checks + mechanical pre-checks. Standard mode still runs a single `_review_prompt()` call that anchors to `finalize.json` (what the plan said to do), not the original issue. We've validated that the questions standard mode SHOULD be asking are already present in the critique phase (`megaplan/checks.py`) but are gated to extended tier or run only against the plan, not the post-execute diff.

This plan is a targeted, **cheap** lift of standard mode quality. No new LLM calls in standard mode. Light mode stays exactly as today. Heavy mode mechanical pre-checks are preserved.

## Goal

Three changes that make standard-mode critique and review materially more thorough without adding API cost:

1. **Run mechanical pre-checks in both standard and heavy review**, not only heavy. Attach as context in standard; auto-block clear failures.
2. **Add a "re-verify critique flags against the final diff" section** to the standard `_review_prompt()` so the single review call re-checks whether pre-execute concerns were actually resolved by the post-execute code.
3. **Re-tier critique checks**: promote `all_locations` and `callers` from extended to core, demote `criteria_quality` from extended-core ordering (or remove from core if it's there). Standard-mode critique becomes substantially more thorough at zero extra cost per check since it's a swap, not an addition.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review` — branches on robustness. Standard path runs the legacy single-call worker. Heavy path calls `run_pre_checks` then `run_parallel_review`. The heavy branch at ~line 1251 calls `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))`.
- `megaplan/review_mechanical.py::run_pre_checks` — returns a list of advisory flag dicts. Runs instantly (no LLM). Safe to call multiple times.
- `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt` — the single-call standard review prompt. Produces criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts/review_verdict/rework_items. Must stay byte-identical in light mode but can gain new sections in standard mode.
- `megaplan/checks.py::CRITIQUE_CHECKS` — 8 critique checks with `tier` in {"core", "extended"}. Standard robustness runs core only via `checks_for_robustness("standard")`. Heavy runs all 8.
- `megaplan/_core/flags.py` (or wherever flag registry lives) — the flag registry with open/verified/disputed status per flag. Already accessible from review.
- `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py` — review schema. Already widened in the previous robust-review megaplan to accept `pre_check_flags`. The standard review payload must remain backward compatible.

## What needs to exist

### 1. Run mechanical pre-checks in standard mode too

In `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review`, move the `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))` call out of the heavy branch and run it **unconditionally** before both the standard and heavy review paths.

- For heavy: keep the existing flow — `pre_check_flags` becomes the input to `run_parallel_review`, gets merged into `merged_payload["pre_check_flags"]`.
- For standard: after `run_pre_checks` returns, filter for **blocking** flags (clear failures like `source_touch` reporting a test-only diff, or `dead_guard_static` reporting an unreachable guard in a new source branch). If any blocking flag exists, short-circuit: write a minimal `review.json` with `review_verdict="needs_rework"`, populate `rework_items` with one entry per blocking mechanical flag, set `pre_check_flags` to the full list, and skip the LLM review call entirely. This is a free auto-block.
- For standard with no blocking mechanical flags: attach `pre_check_flags` to the existing review payload as context for the single review call. Pass them into `_review_prompt` as an extra block so the LLM can see them when scoring criteria. Populate `payload["pre_check_flags"]` from the returned worker result.
- Light mode: do NOT run mechanical pre-checks. Light mode stays exactly as today.

Decide which pre-check findings are "blocking" via a helper:
- `source_touch` with severity `significant` and "only touches test-like files" detail → blocking
- `dead_guard_static` with `UNREACHABLE` in the id → blocking (requires a new severity tier or id marker since current implementation only reports parse failures)
- `diff_size_sanity` → never blocking (advisory only)

If the distinction is hard to make cleanly, the simpler alternative is: in standard mode, all mechanical flags are advisory only and attached as prompt context; no auto-blocking. Auto-blocking can come in a later PR. Implementer's call — pick the simpler option if the blocking logic requires more than a single-function helper.

### 2. Re-verify critique flags against final diff in standard review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt`, add a new section between the existing requirements block and the JSON shape spec. The new section should:

- Read the flag registry via `load_flag_registry(plan_dir)`.
- Filter for flags with status `open`, `verified`, or `disputed` (not `resolved`).
- For each such flag, include its id, concern text, severity, and current status in the prompt.
- Instruct the model: "For each flag below that was raised during critique, verify whether the final diff actually addresses the concern. A flag is resolved if the final diff contains code that directly addresses the concern. If a flag is not resolved in the final diff, add a `rework_item` referencing the flag id and explaining what's still missing. Do not trust pre-execute promises — check the diff itself."
- The output `rework_items` gain a new optional field `flag_id` pointing to the critique flag id. Existing `source` field should be set to `"review_flag_reverify"` for any rework item synthesized this way.

Schema impact: add optional `flag_id` field to rework_items shape in both `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py`. Keep it optional so existing payloads still validate.

The new prompt section only runs if there are open/verified/disputed flags in the registry; if the registry is empty or all resolved, the section is omitted entirely (keeps the standard prompt byte-identical for plans without critique flags, which is important for the existing byte-identical regression test in `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`).

This must not change light mode. Light mode doesn't run critique at all and has no flag registry, so a "no flags → no section" gate handles this automatically.

### 3. Re-tier critique checks

In `megaplan/checks.py`, in `CRITIQUE_CHECKS`:

- Promote `all_locations` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Promote `callers` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Demote `criteria_quality` from `tier="core"` to `tier="extended"` (if it's core — verify first). The reasoning: `criteria_quality` is about whether success criteria are well-formed, which is plan hygiene. `all_locations` and `callers` are about whether the plan actually solves the bug, which is more important for standard mode.

This is a **net-zero change** in number of checks per mode:
- Standard robustness was running 4 core checks, will still run 4 (swap `criteria_quality` out, swap `all_locations` in). Net neutral, and if `callers` is also promoted, standard gains one check — verify against the brief's "no new cost per check" claim and either: (a) accept a 1-check increase in standard (minor cost bump), or (b) also demote one more current core check to keep count at 4. The simpler choice is (a) — 5 core checks in standard is fine.
- Heavy robustness is unchanged in total count (still 8 checks, same set, just different tier labels).

Update `megaplan/checks.py:140-144` (or wherever the `checks_for_robustness()` function lives) — no code change should be needed since it filters by tier; only the tier labels on the check entries change.

Update any tests that assert the specific set of core checks to match the new tiering.

### 4. Tests

- **`tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`**:
  - Add a test that standard mode calls `run_pre_checks` before the single review worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with a blocking mechanical flag (if auto-blocking is implemented) short-circuits and writes a `needs_rework` review.json without calling the worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with no blocking mechanical flags attaches `pre_check_flags` to the payload.
  - The existing byte-identical standard-mode fixture test should either continue passing (if no flag registry present) or be updated to expect the new `pre_check_flags` key in the payload. Pick whichever is less churn; document the choice in the test docstring.

- **`tests/test_prompts.py`**:
  - Add a grep-assertion test that `_review_prompt` output includes the flag-reverify section when a fixture flag registry has open flags.
  - Add a symmetric test that the section is absent when the registry is empty (byte-identical backward compat).

- **`tests/test_checks.py`**:
  - Update the expected core/extended tier sets to match the new tiering.
  - Assert `all_locations` and `callers` are in core.
  - Assert `criteria_quality` is in extended.

- **`tests/test_schemas.py`**:
  - Add a test case for `rework_items[].flag_id` (optional string).

- **Regression**: full suite should still pass. Critically: `tests/test_parallel_critique.py`, `tests/test_parallel_review.py`, `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`, `tests/test_review_checks.py`, `tests/test_review_mechanical.py`.

## Out of scope

- Any changes to `run_parallel_review`, `review_checks.py`, or `review_mechanical.py` internals.
- Any changes to heavy mode behavior beyond preserving the existing pre-check plumbing.
- Any changes to light mode.
- New LLM-based checks.
- Changes to critique check guidance text.
- Auto-blocking in standard mode based on mechanical flags — implement if easy, skip if not.

## Success criteria

- Standard mode runs `run_pre_checks` and attaches `pre_check_flags` to its review payload.
- Standard mode's review prompt includes a flag-reverify section when open critique flags exist.
- `all_locations` and `callers` are now core-tier critique checks.
- Light mode review is byte-identical to today.

  "meta_commentary": "Three coordinated changes to the standard-mode review path. Order matters: do T1 (label-only tier swap, instant feedback via T5) and T2 (schema) first because they're free; then T3 (`_review_prompt` extension) which T4 depends on; then T4 (handler wiring + persistence). Land tests alongside their corresponding code change, not at the end \u2014 the per-step regression catches issues early.\n\nCritical correctness pitfalls: (1) The re-verify filter MUST include `addressed` status (FLAG-001) \u2014 without it the feature is silently broken because `update_flags_after_revise` writes that exact status. (2) Standard branch MUST call `update_flags_after_review` (FLAG-002), light mode must NOT \u2014 this asymmetry preserves byte-identity. (3) Re-tiering grows standard critique from 4\u21925 LLM calls (not 6) because `verification` is demoted as compensation; `criteria_quality` is already extended (D3). (4) `tests/test_parallel_critique.py:118-120` will break on the count change (FLAG-004) \u2014 fix proactively in T6, don't wait for it to fail.\n\nByte-identity contract for light mode is load-bearing and tested two ways: T8(c) snapshots `_review_prompt` output, T9(b) snapshots `review.json` shape. If either fails, the fix is in the gating logic (T3's `extra_sections` empty-string path or T4's `if robustness == 'standard'` guards), NOT in the test.\n\nWorker plumbing in T4 step 5 is the riskiest unknown. Investigate `megaplan/workers.py` first to confirm whether `_review_prompt` is called with kwargs or via a fixed signature. Sidecar fallback (`pre_check_flags.json` next to the registry) is acceptable per the plan and only adds one read; don't waste cycles on a refactor.\n\n`update_flags_after_review` may expect `payload['checks']` \u2014 standard payloads don't have it. Verify this is a soft-fail/no-op before assuming it works. If it raises, the minimal fix is a `payload.setdefault('checks', [])` or equivalent guard at the call site.\n\nAuto-blocking on mechanical flags in standard mode is explicitly deferred (D1) \u2014 leave a one-line comment at the attachment site noting this. Don't be tempted to add it.\n\nFinal validation in T10 must run the focused tests first (fast feedback), then brief-mandated regressions, then full suite. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. gate = read_json(plan_dir / \"gate.json\")\n+    finalize_data = read_json(plan_dir / \"finalize.json\")\n+    settled_decisions_block = _settled_decisions_block(gate)\n+    settled_decisions_instruction = _settled_decisions_instruction(gate)\n+    diff_summary = collect_git_diff_summary(project_dir)\n+    audit_path = plan_dir / \"execution_audit.json\"\n+    if audit_path.exists():\n+        audit_block = textwrap.dedent(\n+            f\"\"\"\n+            Execution audit (`execution_audit.json`):\n+            {json_dump(read_json(audit_path)).strip()}\n+            \"\"\"\n+        ).strip()\n+    else:\n+        audit_block = \"Execution audit (`execution_audit.json`): not present. Skip that artifact gracefully and rely on `finalize.json`, `execution.json`, and the git diff.\"\n+    return textwrap.dedent(\n+        f\"\"\"\n+        Review the implementation against the success criteria.\n+\n+        Project directory:\n+        {project_dir}\n+\n+        {intent_and_notes_block(state)}\n+\n+        Approved plan:\n+        {latest_plan}\n+\n+        Execution tracking state (`finalize.json`):\n+        {json_dump(finalize_data).strip()}\n+\n+        Plan metadata:\n+        {json_dump(latest_meta).strip()}\n+\n+        Gate summary:\n+        {json_dump(gate).strip()}\n+\n+        {settled_decisions_block}\n+\n+        Execution summary:\n+        {json_dump(execution).strip()}\n+\n+        {audit_block}\n+\n+        Git diff summary:\n+        {diff_summary}\n+\n+        Requirements:\n+        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.\n+        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.\n+        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:\n+          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.\n+          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.\n+          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.\n+          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.\n+        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.\n+        {settled_decisions_instruction}\n+        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.\n+        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.\n+        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:\n+        ```json\n+        {{\n+          \"review_verdict\": \"approved\",\n+          \"criteria\": [\n+            {{\n+              \"name\": \"All existing tests pass\",\n+              \"priority\": \"must\",\n+              \"pass\": \"pass\",\n+              \"evidence\": \"Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed.\"\n+            }},\n+            {{\n+              \"name\": \"File under ~300 lines\",\n+              \"priority\": \"should\",\n+              \"pass\": \"pass\",\n+              \"evidence\": \"File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met.\"\n+            }},\n+            {{\n+              \"name\": \"Manual smoke tests pass\",\n+              \"priority\": \"info\",\n+              \"pass\": \"waived\",\n+              \"evidence\": \"Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA.\"\n+            }}\n+          ],\n+          \"issues\": [],\n+          \"rework_items\": [],\n+          \"summary\": \"Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.\",\n+          \"task_verdicts\": [\n+            {{\n+              \"task_id\": \"T6\",\n+              \"reviewer_verdict\": \"Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.\",\n+              \"evidence_files\": [\"megaplan/handlers.py\", \"megaplan/evaluation.py\"]\n+            }}\n+          ],\n+          \"sense_check_verdicts\": [\n+            {{\n+              \"sense_check_id\": \"SC6\",\n+              \"verdict\": \"Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty.\"\n+            }}\n+          ]\n+        }}\n+        ```\n+        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:\n+          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to\n+          - `issue`: what is wrong\n+          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like\n+          - `actual`: what was observed\n+          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding\n+        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.\n+        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.\n+        \"\"\"\n+    ).strip()\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
# Lift Standard Review Quality via Mechanical Pre-checks, Flag Re-verification, and Critique Re-tiering

## Context

Megaplan has three robustness modes: `light`, `standard`, `heavy`. Heavy mode just shipped a new parallel `run_parallel_review` with 4 focused LLM checks + mechanical pre-checks. Standard mode still runs a single `_review_prompt()` call that anchors to `finalize.json` (what the plan said to do), not the original issue. We've validated that the questions standard mode SHOULD be asking are already present in the critique phase (`megaplan/checks.py`) but are gated to extended tier or run only against the plan, not the post-execute diff.

This plan is a targeted, **cheap** lift of standard mode quality. No new LLM calls in standard mode. Light mode stays exactly as today. Heavy mode mechanical pre-checks are preserved.

## Goal

Three changes that make standard-mode critique and review materially more thorough without adding API cost:

1. **Run mechanical pre-checks in both standard and heavy review**, not only heavy. Attach as context in standard; auto-block clear failures.
2. **Add a "re-verify critique flags against the final diff" section** to the standard `_review_prompt()` so the single review call re-checks whether pre-execute concerns were actually resolved by the post-execute code.
3. **Re-tier critique checks**: promote `all_locations` and `callers` from extended to core, demote `criteria_quality` from extended-core ordering (or remove from core if it's there). Standard-mode critique becomes substantially more thorough at zero extra cost per check since it's a swap, not an addition.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review` — branches on robustness. Standard path runs the legacy single-call worker. Heavy path calls `run_pre_checks` then `run_parallel_review`. The heavy branch at ~line 1251 calls `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))`.
- `megaplan/review_mechanical.py::run_pre_checks` — returns a list of advisory flag dicts. Runs instantly (no LLM). Safe to call multiple times.
- `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt` — the single-call standard review prompt. Produces criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts/review_verdict/rework_items. Must stay byte-identical in light mode but can gain new sections in standard mode.
- `megaplan/checks.py::CRITIQUE_CHECKS` — 8 critique checks with `tier` in {"core", "extended"}. Standard robustness runs core only via `checks_for_robustness("standard")`. Heavy runs all 8.
- `megaplan/_core/flags.py` (or wherever flag registry lives) — the flag registry with open/verified/disputed status per flag. Already accessible from review.
- `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py` — review schema. Already widened in the previous robust-review megaplan to accept `pre_check_flags`. The standard review payload must remain backward compatible.

## What needs to exist

### 1. Run mechanical pre-checks in standard mode too

In `megaplan/handlers.py::handle_review`, move the `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]))` call out of the heavy branch and run it **unconditionally** before both the standard and heavy review paths.

- For heavy: keep the existing flow — `pre_check_flags` becomes the input to `run_parallel_review`, gets merged into `merged_payload["pre_check_flags"]`.
- For standard: after `run_pre_checks` returns, filter for **blocking** flags (clear failures like `source_touch` reporting a test-only diff, or `dead_guard_static` reporting an unreachable guard in a new source branch). If any blocking flag exists, short-circuit: write a minimal `review.json` with `review_verdict="needs_rework"`, populate `rework_items` with one entry per blocking mechanical flag, set `pre_check_flags` to the full list, and skip the LLM review call entirely. This is a free auto-block.
- For standard with no blocking mechanical flags: attach `pre_check_flags` to the existing review payload as context for the single review call. Pass them into `_review_prompt` as an extra block so the LLM can see them when scoring criteria. Populate `payload["pre_check_flags"]` from the returned worker result.
- Light mode: do NOT run mechanical pre-checks. Light mode stays exactly as today.

Decide which pre-check findings are "blocking" via a helper:
- `source_touch` with severity `significant` and "only touches test-like files" detail → blocking
- `dead_guard_static` with `UNREACHABLE` in the id → blocking (requires a new severity tier or id marker since current implementation only reports parse failures)
- `diff_size_sanity` → never blocking (advisory only)

If the distinction is hard to make cleanly, the simpler alternative is: in standard mode, all mechanical flags are advisory only and attached as prompt context; no auto-blocking. Auto-blocking can come in a later PR. Implementer's call — pick the simpler option if the blocking logic requires more than a single-function helper.

### 2. Re-verify critique flags against final diff in standard review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py::_review_prompt`, add a new section between the existing requirements block and the JSON shape spec. The new section should:

- Read the flag registry via `load_flag_registry(plan_dir)`.
- Filter for flags with status `open`, `verified`, or `disputed` (not `resolved`).
- For each such flag, include its id, concern text, severity, and current status in the prompt.
- Instruct the model: "For each flag below that was raised during critique, verify whether the final diff actually addresses the concern. A flag is resolved if the final diff contains code that directly addresses the concern. If a flag is not resolved in the final diff, add a `rework_item` referencing the flag id and explaining what's still missing. Do not trust pre-execute promises — check the diff itself."
- The output `rework_items` gain a new optional field `flag_id` pointing to the critique flag id. Existing `source` field should be set to `"review_flag_reverify"` for any rework item synthesized this way.

Schema impact: add optional `flag_id` field to rework_items shape in both `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and `megaplan/schemas.py`. Keep it optional so existing payloads still validate.

The new prompt section only runs if there are open/verified/disputed flags in the registry; if the registry is empty or all resolved, the section is omitted entirely (keeps the standard prompt byte-identical for plans without critique flags, which is important for the existing byte-identical regression test in `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`).

This must not change light mode. Light mode doesn't run critique at all and has no flag registry, so a "no flags → no section" gate handles this automatically.

### 3. Re-tier critique checks

In `megaplan/checks.py`, in `CRITIQUE_CHECKS`:

- Promote `all_locations` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Promote `callers` from `tier="extended"` to `tier="core"`.
- Demote `criteria_quality` from `tier="core"` to `tier="extended"` (if it's core — verify first). The reasoning: `criteria_quality` is about whether success criteria are well-formed, which is plan hygiene. `all_locations` and `callers` are about whether the plan actually solves the bug, which is more important for standard mode.

This is a **net-zero change** in number of checks per mode:
- Standard robustness was running 4 core checks, will still run 4 (swap `criteria_quality` out, swap `all_locations` in). Net neutral, and if `callers` is also promoted, standard gains one check — verify against the brief's "no new cost per check" claim and either: (a) accept a 1-check increase in standard (minor cost bump), or (b) also demote one more current core check to keep count at 4. The simpler choice is (a) — 5 core checks in standard is fine.
- Heavy robustness is unchanged in total count (still 8 checks, same set, just different tier labels).

Update `megaplan/checks.py:140-144` (or wherever the `checks_for_robustness()` function lives) — no code change should be needed since it filters by tier; only the tier labels on the check entries change.

Update any tests that assert the specific set of core checks to match the new tiering.

### 4. Tests

- **`tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`**:
  - Add a test that standard mode calls `run_pre_checks` before the single review worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with a blocking mechanical flag (if auto-blocking is implemented) short-circuits and writes a `needs_rework` review.json without calling the worker.
  - Add a test that standard mode with no blocking mechanical flags attaches `pre_check_flags` to the payload.
  - The existing byte-identical standard-mode fixture test should either continue passing (if no flag registry present) or be updated to expect the new `pre_check_flags` key in the payload. Pick whichever is less churn; document the choice in the test docstring.

- **`tests/test_prompts.py`**:
  - Add a grep-assertion test that `_review_prompt` output includes the flag-reverify section when a fixture flag registry has open flags.
  - Add a symmetric test that the section is absent when the registry is empty (byte-identical backward compat).

- **`tests/test_checks.py`**:
  - Update the expected core/extended tier sets to match the new tiering.
  - Assert `all_locations` and `callers` are in core.
  - Assert `criteria_quality` is in extended.

- **`tests/test_schemas.py`**:
  - Add a test case for `rework_items[].flag_id` (optional string).

- **Regression**: full suite should still pass. Critically: `tests/test_parallel_critique.py`, `tests/test_parallel_review.py`, `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py`, `tests/test_review_checks.py`, `tests/test_review_mechanical.py`.

## Out of scope

- Any changes to `run_parallel_review`, `review_checks.py`, or `review_mechanical.py` internals.
- Any changes to heavy mode behavior beyond preserving the existing pre-check plumbing.
- Any changes to light mode.
- New LLM-based checks.
- Changes to critique check guidance text.
- Auto-blocking in standard mode based on mechanical flags — implement if easy, skip if not.

## Success criteria

- Standard mode runs `run_pre_checks` and attaches `pre_check_flags` to its review payload.
- Standard mode's review prompt includes a flag-reverify section when open critique flags exist.
- `all_locations` and `callers` are now core-tier critique checks.
- Light mode review is byte-identical to today.
- Heavy mode review is unchanged in structure.
- All existing tests pass; new tests for the three changes pass.
- No new LLM calls are added to standard mode — the changes are: (a) mechanical pre-checks (free), (b) a prompt section (no new call), (c) check re-tiering (swap, not add).

## Files likely to change

- `megaplan/handlers.py` — `handle_review` changes
- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — `_review_prompt` changes
- `megaplan/checks.py` — tier re-labeling
- `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` — optional `flag_id` in rework_items
- `megaplan/schemas.py` — mirror the schema change
- `tests/test_handle_review_robustness.py` — new tests
- `tests/test_prompts.py` — new grep-assertions
- `tests/test_checks.py` — updated tier assertions
- `tests/test_schemas.py` — flag_id schema test

(4) `tests/test_parallel_critique.py:118-120` will break on the count change (FLAG-004) \u2014 fix proactively in T6, don't wait for it to fail.\n\nByte-identity contract for light mode is load-bearing and tested two ways: T8(c) snapshots `_review_prompt` output, T9(b) snapshots `review.json` shape.         Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. - [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. - [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname="hidden md:block"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.
